At 80% of a limit VibeSwitcher starts no workers or audits on any model, so the limit doesn't stop one mid-task; sessions already running go on. Tell the user. Nothing on it starts by itself until the reset, then it does; start_worker or audit with override_usage: true starts one anyway. A bounded, mechanical task with a tight contract can run on Codex instead (update_task(<task>, agent: codex): the Codex account's usage, always audited by a Claude verifier); you decide.

- 2026-11-08 05:45 The worker on host-events-decode-off answered findings: host-events-decode-off:F1 fixed at e455bcee: 9696ca8a (tests tightened in e455bcee): the reader keeps a count, under the `sending` lock, of what it has handed to `queue` but not yet delivered, in bytes and batches. When that passes 4 MB or 64 batches, it suspends the read source and stops its loop. Each delivered batch lowers the count, and the source resumes once at most half the bound is still waiting, so the host's bytes wait in the socket and the host holds back as it did before. The suspend…

A worker's shell, a verifier's command or a branch's tests can write it (the deny rules hold only the file tools), and the next session would load what was written. New workers, verifiers and merges are held until the user accepts the change in VibeSwitcher (its notice names the files); you can't. Tell the user which changed and ask them to look: if they made the change, they accept it there; if not, they set it back first.
